## GHSA-6wcc-39rp-hh9p · @hypequery/clickhouse

2026-07-28 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→SQL`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `escapeValue` function, which is responsible for sanitizing string inputs before they are used in SQL queries, did not properly escape backslash characters. While it correctly handled single quotes by doubling them, an attacker could use backslashes to bypass this escaping mechanism and inject arbitrary SQL.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary SQL commands into queries, potentially leading to unauthorized data access, modification, or deletion, and even remote code execution on the underlying database server.

### Key diff

```
-	    return `'${value.replace(/'/g, "''")}'`;
+	    const escaped = value.replace(/\\/g, '\\\\').replace(/'/g, "''");
+	    return `'${escaped}'`;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `escapeValue` function to correctly handle backslash characters in string inputs. It now replaces each backslash with a double backslash (`\\`) before replacing single quotes, ensuring that backslashes cannot be used to escape the single quote delimiter.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 28 occurrences**

## GHSA-386q-5hp3-95m9 · datamodel-code-generator

2026-07-28 · Python · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `datamodel-code-generator` library allowed arbitrary string values for the `default_factory` field in a JSON schema. This field is used to generate Python code, and if an attacker could control its value, they could inject malicious Python code that would be executed when the generated model was used.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the system generating the Python data models by providing a specially crafted JSON schema with a malicious `default_factory` value.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/datamodel_code_generator/parser/jsonschema.py
+++ b/src/datamodel_code_generator/parser/jsonschema.py
@@ -809,6 +820,8 @@ def get_field_extras(self, obj: JsonSchemaObject) -> dict[str, Any]:
             }
         if self.default_field_extras:
             extras.update(self.default_field_extras)
+        if (default_factory := extras.get("default_factory", UNDEFINED)) is not UNDEFINED:
+            extras["default_factory"] = _validate_default_factory(default_factory)
         return extras
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a validation function `_validate_default_factory` that restricts the allowed values for `default_factory` to a predefined safe set (e.g., 'dict', 'list', 'set'). Any other value will now raise an error, preventing code injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 85 occurrences**

## GHSA-vj5c-m527-mpff · style-dictionary

2026-07-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.8 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the `convertTokenData` utility function, which was intended to convert token data between different formats (object, array, map). The original implementation likely used an unsafe deep merge or object assignment operation without proper validation or sanitization of input keys, allowing an attacker to inject arbitrary properties into the Object.prototype.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the prototype of JavaScript objects, potentially leading to denial of service, remote code execution, or other unexpected behavior depending on how the polluted properties are later used in the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/StyleDictionary.js
+++ b/lib/StyleDictionary.js
@@ -361,7 +364,9 @@ export default class StyleDictionary extends Register {
       preprocessedTokens = expandTokens(preprocessedTokens, this.options);
     }
     this.tokens = preprocessedTokens;
-    this.allTokens = flattenTokens(/** @type {PreprocessedTokens} */ (this.tokens), this.usesDtcg);
+    this.allTokens = convertTokenData(this.tokens, { output: 'array', usesDtcg: this.usesDtcg });
+    this.tokenMap = convertTokenData(this.allTokens, { output: 'map', usesDtcg: this.usesDtcg });
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `convertTokenData` utility function and refactors `StyleDictionary.js` to use it for managing token data. This new utility function likely implements safer object manipulation, preventing prototype pollution by carefully handling how token data is converted and stored, specifically when converting to an array or map format.

---

## GHSA-qf5v-m7p4-95rp · github.com/fission/fission

2026-07-28 · Go · **HIGH** 8.5 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The system used an incomplete denylist for Linux capabilities in container security contexts, allowing tenants to add dangerous capabilities like CAP_SYS_TIME. This was particularly problematic in environments where admission webhooks could be bypassed, leaving the merge-layer sanitization as the only defense, which was insufficient.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve cross-tenant node wall-clock corruption by leveraging capabilities like CAP_SYS_TIME, potentially disrupting services or affecting time-sensitive operations across different tenants.

### Key diff

```
-	var dangerousMergeContainerCapabilities = map[apiv1.Capability]struct{}{
-		"SYS_ADMIN":       {},
-		"NET_ADMIN":       {},
-		"SYS_PTRACE":      {},
-		"SYS_MODULE":      {},
-		"DAC_READ_SEARCH": {},
-		"DAC_OVERRIDE":    {},
+	var allowedMergeContainerCapabilities = map[apiv1.Capability]struct{}{
+		"NET_BIND_SERVICE": {},
 	}
 
 	// sanitizeContainerSecurityContext zeroes out the privilege-escalation bits
-			if _, bad := dangerousMergeContainerCapabilities[cap]; bad {
+			if _, ok := allowedMergeContainerCapabilities[cap]; !ok {
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes the security context sanitization from a denylist to a strict allowlist for Linux capabilities. It also adds new CEL admission gate rules to explicitly disallow hostNetwork, hostPID, hostIPC, and serviceAccountName/serviceAccount overrides in Function and Environment PodSpecs.

## GHSA-28f5-38xr-jh2w · io.appium:java-client

2026-07-28 · Java · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The AppiumCommandExecutor in java-client allowed an unvalidated URL redirect via the `directConnect` feature. An attacker could specify a malicious URL that, when resolved, pointed to internal network resources, loopback addresses, or cloud metadata services, leading to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF).

### Impact

An attacker could force the Appium server to make requests to arbitrary internal network resources, potentially leading to information disclosure (e.g., cloud metadata, internal service banners) or interaction with internal services, thereby pivoting within the network.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/io/appium/java_client/remote/AppiumCommandExecutor.java
+++ b/src/main/java/io/appium/java_client/remote/AppiumCommandExecutor.java
@@ -155,5 +156,12 @@ protected HttpClient getClient() {
      * @param serverUrl A url to override.
+     * @param serverUrl URL to use for subsequent HTTP requests. Before switching clients, the host is
+     *                  resolved and the override is refused if any resolved address is loopback,
+     *                  link-local (including IPv4 link-local such as metadata-service ranges),
+     *                  unspecified ({@code 0.0.0.0} / {@code ::}), or multicast. Private (RFC 1918),
+     *                  carrier-grade NAT ({@code 100.64.0.0/10}), IPv6 unique-local ({@code fc00::/7}),
+     *                  and other non-special addresses are not rejected by this check.
      */
     protected void overrideServerUrl(URL serverUrl) {
+        DirectConnectUrlSafety.requireSafeOverrideTarget(serverUrl);
         HttpClient newClient = getHttpClientFactory().createClient(appiumClientConfig.baseUrl(serverUrl));
         setPrivateFieldValue(HttpCommandExecutor.class, "client", newClient);
```

### Fix summary

A new utility class, `DirectConnectUrlSafety`, was introduced to validate URLs used for server overrides. This class checks if the resolved IP address of the provided URL is a loopback, link-local, unspecified, or multicast address, and throws an exception if it is, preventing connections to such disallowed targets.

## GHSA-rfr2-mq9m-x2qx · datamodel-code-generator

2026-07-28 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The `datamodel-code-generator` library, when fetching schema definitions from a URL, did not validate the hostname or IP address of the provided URL. Additionally, it automatically followed HTTP redirects without re-validating the redirected URL. This allowed an attacker to specify a URL pointing to an internal network resource or a cloud metadata endpoint, or to redirect to such resources.

### Impact

An attacker could force the application to make requests to arbitrary internal network resources, potentially accessing sensitive information, internal services, or cloud metadata endpoints, leading to information disclosure or further network compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/datamodel_code_generator/http.py
+++ b/src/datamodel_code_generator/http.py
@@ -26,36 +29,139 @@ def _get_httpx() -> Any:
 
 
 DEFAULT_HTTP_TIMEOUT = 30.0
+MAX_HTTP_REDIRECTS = 20
+_HTTP_REDIRECT_STATUS_CODES = frozenset({301, 302, 303, 307, 308})
+_UNSAFE_HOST_NAMES = frozenset({"localhost"})
 
 
-def get_body(
+def _is_safe_ip(ip: IPv4Address | IPv6Address) -> bool:
+    return ip.is_global
+
+
+def _get_ips_from_host(host: str) -> tuple[IPv4Address | IPv6Address, ...]:
+    try:
+        return (ip_address(host.split("%", maxsplit=1)[0]),)
+    except ValueError:
+        pass
+
+    try:
+        addr_infos = socket.getaddrinfo(host, None, type=socket.SOCK_STREAM)
+    except OSError:
+        return ()
+
+    return tuple({ip_address(str(addr_info[4][0]).split("%", maxsplit=1)[0]) for addr_info in addr_infos})
+
+
+def _format_private_network_error(
+    *,
+    url: str,
+    hostname: str,
+    reason: str,
+    resolved_ips: tuple[IPv4Address | IPv6Address, ...] = (),
+) -> str:
+    ip_details = f" Resolved IPs: {', '.join(str(ip) for ip in resolved_ips)}." if resolved_ips else ""
+    return (
+        f"Blocked unsafe URL host: {hostname}\n"
+        f"Reason: {reason}.{ip_details}\n"
+        "datamodel-code-generator blocks local, private, link-local, reserved, and otherwise non-public "
+        "network targets by default to reduce SSRF risk.\n"
+        f"URL: {url}\n"
+        "If this is a trusted internal schema endpoint, pass --allow-private-network "
+        "or set allow_private_network=True when using the Python API."
+    )
+
+
+def _validate_url_for_fetch(url: str, *, allow_private_network: bool) -> None:
+    parsed_url = urlparse(url)
+    match parsed_url.scheme:
+        case "http" | "https":
+            pass
+        case _:
+            msg = f"Unsupported URL scheme for HTTP fetch: {url}"
+            raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
+
+    if (hostname := parsed_url.hostname) is None:
+        msg = f"Missing URL host for HTTP fetch: {url}"
+        raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
+
+    host = hostname.rstrip(".").lower()
+    if allow_private_network:
+        return
+
+    if host in _UNSAFE_HOST_NAMES or host.endswith(".localhost"):
+        msg = _format_private_network_error(
+            url=url,
+            hostname=hostname,
+            reason="localhost names resolve to the local machine",
+        )
+        raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
+
+    ips = _get_ips_from_host(host)
+    if not ips:
+        return
+
+    if all(_is_safe_ip(ip) for ip in ips):
+        return
+
+    msg = _format_private_network_error(
+        url=url,
+        hostname=hostname,
+        reason="the host resolves to a non-public address",
+        resolved_ips=ips,
+    )
+    raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
+
+
+def _get_redirect_url(httpx: Any, current_url: str, response: Any) -> str | None:
+    if response.status_code not in _HTTP_REDIRECT_STATUS_CODES:
+        return None
+    if not (location := response.headers.get("location")):
+        msg = f"Redirect response from {current_url} is missing a Location header"
+        raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
+    return str(httpx.URL(current_url).join(location))
+
+
+def get_body(  # noqa: PLR0913
     url: str,
     headers: Sequence[tuple[str, str]] | None = None,
     ignore_tls: bool = False,  # noqa: FBT001, FBT002
     query_parameters: Sequence[tuple[str, str]] | None = None,
     timeout: float = DEFAULT_HTTP_TIMEOUT,
+    *,
+    allow_private_network: bool = False,
 ) -> str:
     """Fetch content from a URL with optional headers and query parameters."""
     httpx = _get_httpx()
-    try:
-        response = httpx.get(
-            url,
-            headers=headers,
-            verify=not ignore_tls,
-            follow_redirects=True,
-            params=query_parameters,  # ty: ignore
-            timeout=timeout,
-        )
-    except Exception as e:
-        msg = f"Failed to fetch {url}: {e}"
-        raise SchemaFetchError(msg) from e
+    current_url = url
+    for redirect_count in range(MAX_HTTP_REDIRECTS + 1):
+        _validate_url_for_fetch(current_url, allow_private_network=allow_private_network)
+        try:
+            response = httpx.get(
+                current_url,
+                headers=headers,
+                verify=not ignore_tls,
+                follow_redirects=False,
+                params=query_parameters if redirect_count == 0 else None,  # ty: ignore
+                timeout=timeout,
+            )
+        except Exception as e:
+            msg = f"Failed to fetch {current_url}: {e}"
+            raise SchemaFetchError(msg) from e
+        if (redirect_url := _get_redirect_url(httpx, current_url, response)) is None:
+            break
+        current_url = redirect_url
+    else:
+        msg = f"Too many redirects fetching {url}"
+        raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
+
     if response.status_code >= 400:  # noqa: PLR2004
-        msg = f"HTTP {response.status_code} error fetching {url}"
+        msg = f"HTTP {response.status_code} error fetching {current_url}"
         raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
     content_type = response.headers.get("content-type", "").lower()
     if "text/html" in content_type:
         msg = (
-            f"Unexpected HTML response from {url} (Content-Type: {content_type}). Expected JSON or YAML schema content."
+            f"Unexpected HTML response from {current_url} "
+            f"(Content-Type: {content_type}). Expected JSON or YAML schema content."
         )
         raise SchemaFetchError(msg)
     return response.text
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces validation for URLs before fetching, checking for schemes, hostnames, and resolved IP addresses to prevent access to private, local, or reserved networks. It also disables automatic redirect following and manually handles redirects, applying the same validation to each redirected URL. A new `--allow-private-network` option is added for trusted internal use cases.

## GHSA-8r6w-3qq5-4p4r · github.com/pterodactyl/wings

2026-07-28 · Go · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The system was generating JSON Web Tokens (JWTs) for various operations without explicitly defining and enforcing specific scopes. This allowed subusers, who might not have been granted explicit 'file.create' permissions, to generate JWTs that were implicitly trusted by the backend for file upload operations, effectively bypassing intended access controls.

### Impact

An attacker, specifically a subuser, could upload files to a server even if they were not explicitly granted the 'file.create' permission, leading to unauthorized file manipulation and potential system compromise or data integrity issues.

### Key diff

```
--- a/app/Services/Nodes/NodeJWTService.php
+++ b/app/Services/Nodes/NodeJWTService.php
@@ -32,6 +36,13 @@ public function setClaims(array $claims): self
         return $this;
     }
 
+    public function setScopes(JwtScope ...$scopes): self
+    {
+        $this->scopes = $scopes;
+
+        return $this;
+    }
+
     /**
      * Attaches a user to the JWT being created and will automatically inject the
      * "user_uuid" key into the final claims array with the user's UUID.
@@ -85,15 +96,12 @@ public function handle(Node $node, ?string $identifiedBy, string $algo = 'md5'):
             $builder = $builder->withClaim($key, $value);
         }
 
+        Assert::notEmpty($this->scopes, 'Cannot generate a JWT without providing at least one scope.');
+
+        $builder = $builder->withClaim('scope', implode(' ', array_map(fn ($scope) => $scope->value, $this->scopes)));
+
         if (!is_null($this->user)) {
-            $builder = $builder
-                ->withClaim('user_uuid', $this->user->uuid)
-                // The "user_id" claim is deprecated and should not be referenced — it remains
-                // here solely to ensure older versions of Wings are unaffected when the Panel
-                // is updated.
-                //
-                // This claim will be removed in Panel@1.11 or later.
-                ->withClaim('user_id', $this->user->id);
+            $builder = $builder->withClaim('user_uuid', $this->user->uuid);
         }
 
         return $builder
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit JWT scopes using an Enum (JwtScope) and modifies the JWT generation service to require and include these scopes in the generated tokens. All relevant controllers (file upload, file download, server transfer) are updated to explicitly set the required scopes when requesting a JWT, ensuring that tokens are only valid for their intended purpose.

## GHSA-8m8r-38jm-f355 · datamodel-code-generator

2026-07-28 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The application allowed unvalidated user-supplied input from the `--extra-template-data` argument to be directly used in generated Python code, specifically within `field_validator` and `model_validator` definitions. The `function` and `fields` entries in the `validators` configuration were not properly sanitized or validated, leading to arbitrary code injection during the code generation process.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary Python code into the generated models by crafting malicious `validators` entries in the `--extra-template-data`. This code would then be executed when the generated models are imported, leading to remote code execution on the system running the code generator.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/datamodel_code_generator/validators.py
+++ b/src/datamodel_code_generator/validators.py
@@ -27,6 +29,42 @@
     function: str
     mode: ValidatorMode = ValidatorMode.AFTER
 
+    @field_validator("field")
+    @classmethod
+    def validate_field(cls, value: str | None) -> str | None:
+        """Validate a single field name."""
+        if value is None:
+            return value
+        return _validate_python_identifier(value)
+
+    @field_validator("fields")
+    @classmethod
+    def validate_fields(cls, value: list[str] | None) -> list[str] | None:
+        """Validate multiple field names."""
+        if value is None:
+            return value
+        for field_name in value:
+            _validate_python_identifier(field_name)
+        return value
+
+    @field_validator("function")
+    @classmethod
+    def validate_function(cls, value: str) -> str:
+        """Validate the imported validator function path."""
+        return _validate_dotted_python_identifier_path(value)
+
+    @field_validator("mode", mode="before")
+    @classmethod
+    def validate_mode(cls, value: Any) -> Any:
+        """Validate the Pydantic field_validator mode."""
+        if isinstance(value, ValidatorMode):
+            return value
+        if isinstance(value, str) and value in _validator_mode_values():
+            return value
+        allowed_values = ", ".join(repr(mode.value) for mode in ValidatorMode)
+        msg = f"must be one of: {allowed_values}"
+        raise ValueError(msg)
 
 class ModelValidators(BaseModel):
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces strict validation for `validators` configuration, ensuring that `field` names, `fields` lists, and `function` paths are valid Python identifiers or dotted Python identifier paths. It also validates the `mode` of the validator. This prevents arbitrary code from being injected into the generated Python files by sanitizing the input before it's used in code generation.

## GHSA-8359-h9fx-j6v9 · datamodel-code-generator

2026-07-28 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The `datamodel-code-generator` library, when processing JSON-Schema `$ref` fields, did not properly restrict file system access. Even with `--no-allow-remote-refs` enabled, it was possible to use `file://` URIs or relative paths with `../` traversal to read arbitrary local files outside the intended base directory.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious JSON schema that, when processed by the vulnerable application, would read sensitive local files from the system where the application is running, potentially leading to information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/datamodel_code_generator/parser/jsonschema.py
+++ b/src/datamodel_code_generator/parser/jsonschema.py
@@ -4878,9 +4906,9 @@ def _get_ref_body_from_url(self, ref: str) -> dict[str, YamlValue]:
             # Handle UNC paths (file://server/share/path)
             if parsed.netloc:
                 path = f"//{parsed.netloc}{path}"
-            file_path = Path(path)
+            file_path = self._resolve_local_ref_path(Path(path), ref)
             return self.remote_object_cache.get_or_put(
-                ref, default_factory=lambda _: load_data_from_path(file_path, self.encoding)
+                str(file_path), default_factory=lambda _: load_data_from_path(file_path, self.encoding)
             )
         if self.http_local_ref_path is not None and urlparse(ref).scheme in {"http", "https"}:
             return self._get_ref_body_from_local_http_path(ref)
@@ -4890,7 +4918,7 @@ def _get_ref_body_from_url(self, ref: str) -> dict[str, YamlValue]:
 
     def _get_ref_body_from_remote(self, resolved_ref: str) -> dict[str, YamlValue]:
         """Get reference body from a remote file path."""
-        full_path = self.base_path / resolved_ref
+        full_path = self._resolve_local_ref_path(self.base_path / resolved_ref, resolved_ref)
 
         try:
             return self.remote_object_cache.get_or_put(
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `_resolve_local_ref_path` method that explicitly checks if a resolved local file path is within the `base_path` of the input schema. If the path is outside the base path and remote references are not explicitly allowed, it raises an error, preventing path traversal. It also clarifies the handling of `file://` references.

## GHSA-rmxw-pq4x-3fvh · goshs.de/goshs/v2

2026-07-28 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application failed to enforce file-based ACLs for the bulk zip-download route. While individual file paths were sanitized, the authorization checks (ACL enforcement) were not performed for each file requested in a bulk download, allowing unauthenticated access to files that should have been restricted.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could download arbitrary files from the server's webroot, bypassing configured access control lists, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/httpserver/updown.go
+++ b/httpserver/updown.go
@@ -229,12 +230,24 @@ func (fs *FileServer) bulkDownload(w http.ResponseWriter, req *http.Request) {
 		return
 	}
 
-	// Validate each path and collect absolute paths; skip any traversal attempts
+	// Validate each path, enforce .goshs ACL, and collect absolute paths.
+	// ACL checks must happen here, before any response headers are written,
+	// so that applyCustomAuth/handleError can still send a proper HTTP error.
 	for _, file := range files {
 		absPath, err := sanitizePath(fs.Webroot, file)
 		if err != nil {
 			continue
 		}
+		acl, aclErr := fs.findEffectiveACL(filepath.Dir(absPath))
+		if aclErr == nil {
+			if ok := fs.applyCustomAuth(w, req, acl); !ok {
+				return
+			}
+			if slices.Contains(acl.Block, filepath.Base(absPath)) {
+				fs.handleError(w, req, fmt.Errorf("requested file is blocked"), http.StatusNotFound)
+				return
+			}
+		}
 		filesCleaned = append(filesCleaned, absPath)
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces explicit ACL checks for each file requested in the bulk download handler. Before appending a file to the list for download, the system now finds the effective ACL for its directory, applies custom authentication if required, and checks if the file is explicitly blocked by the ACL.

## GHSA-w4q6-qw23-4rg7 · github.com/github/github-mcp-server

2026-07-28 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `NULL_DEREF→CRASH`

### Root cause

The `CompletionsHandler` function did not validate if the `req`, `req.Params`, or `req.Params.Ref` objects were nil before attempting to access their fields. An attacker could send a malformed request with missing parameters, leading to a nil pointer dereference when the code tried to access `req.Params.Ref.Type`.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a nil pointer dereference, causing the server process to crash and resulting in a Denial of Service (DoS) for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/github/server.go
+++ b/pkg/github/server.go
@@ -204,6 +204,9 @@ func NewServer(version, name, title string, opts *mcp.ServerOptions) *mcp.Server
 
 func CompletionsHandler(getClient GetClientFn) func(ctx context.Context, req *mcp.CompleteRequest) (*mcp.CompleteResult, error) {
 	return func(ctx context.Context, req *mcp.CompleteRequest) (*mcp.CompleteResult, error) {
+		if req == nil || req.Params == nil || req.Params.Ref == nil {
+			return nil, fmt.Errorf("missing required parameter: ref")
+		}
 		switch req.Params.Ref.Type {
 		case "ref/resource":
 			if strings.HasPrefix(req.Params.Ref.URI, "repo://") {
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds explicit nil checks at the beginning of the `CompletionsHandler` function. It now verifies that `req`, `req.Params`, and `req.Params.Ref` are not nil. If any of these are missing, it returns an error immediately, preventing the nil pointer dereference.

## GHSA-whmm-qj9r-wvr2 · github.com/gotd/td

2026-07-28 · Go · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `Decode` method in `UnencryptedMessage` did not validate the `dataLen` field read from the incoming buffer before attempting to allocate memory. An attacker could send a maliciously crafted message with a very large `dataLen` value, leading to an attempt to allocate an unbounded amount of memory.

### Impact

An attacker could cause a pre-authentication denial of service by sending a specially crafted message, forcing the server to attempt to allocate an extremely large amount of memory, leading to resource exhaustion and application crash.

### Key diff

```
--- a/proto/unencrypted_message.go
+++ b/proto/unencrypted_message.go
@@ -37,6 +39,15 @@ func (u *UnencryptedMessage) Decode(b *bin.Buffer) error {
 	if err != nil {
 		return err
 	}
+	if dataLen < 0 {
+		return &bin.InvalidLengthError{
+			Length: int(dataLen),
+			Where:  "plaintext message data",
+		}
+	}
+	if int(dataLen) > b.Len() {
+		return errors.Wrap(io.ErrUnexpectedEOF, "consume payload")
+	}
 	u.MessageData = append(u.MessageData[:0], make([]byte, dataLen)...)
 	if err := b.ConsumeN(u.MessageData, int(dataLen)); err != nil
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds validation checks for the `dataLen` field. It now explicitly checks if `dataLen` is negative or if the requested length exceeds the available buffer length. If either condition is met, an error is returned, preventing unbounded memory allocation.

## GHSA-7wpj-vvmv-pgm8 · @wakaru/cli

2026-07-28 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application did not properly sanitize or validate filenames provided during the bundle unpack process. An attacker could craft filenames containing path traversal sequences (e.g., '..') or absolute paths, which would then be joined with the intended output directory, allowing files to be written outside of the designated directory.

### Impact

An attacker could write arbitrary files to any location on the file system accessible by the application, potentially leading to remote code execution, denial of service, or other system compromises.

### Key diff

```
-                let out_path = deduplicate_path(&out_dir.join(filename), &mut seen);
-                (out_path, code.as_str())
+                let out_path = resolve_unpack_output_path(&out_dir, filename, &mut seen)?;
+                Ok((out_path, code.as_str()))
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new functions like `safe_relative_module_path`, `canonicalize_unpack_output_path`, and `ensure_path_inside_output_dir` to strictly validate and canonicalize output paths. It explicitly rejects filenames containing parent directory components ('..'), root directory components, or absolute paths, ensuring all unpacked files are written strictly within the specified output directory.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 52 occurrences**

## GHSA-hc4m-q9jh-xw4j · nono-cli

2026-07-28 · RUST · **MODERATE** 6.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The application failed to enforce the presence of a trust bundle for package verification. If a package had a trust bundle, the system would attempt to verify it against a pinned signer from the lockfile. However, if the lockfile entry or its provenance data was missing, the verification would fail open, allowing an unverified package to be considered valid.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious package without a corresponding lockfile entry or provenance data, and the system would fail to properly verify its authenticity, potentially leading to the execution of untrusted code.

### Key diff

```
-        if bundle_path.exists() {
-            let pinned_signer = match locked {
-                None => {
-                    return Err(nono::NonoError::PackageVerification {
-                        package: pack_ref.clone(),
-                        reason: format!(
-                            "pack '{}' has a trust bundle but no lockfile entry — \
-                             reinstall with: nono pull {} --force",
-                            pack_ref, pack_ref
-                        ),
-                    });
-                }
-                Some(pkg) => pkg
-                    .provenance
-                    .as_ref()
-                    .map(|p| p.signer_identity.as_str())
-                    .ok_or_else(|| nono::NonoError::PackageVerification {
-                        package: pack_ref.clone(),
-                        reason: format!(
-                            "pack '{}' has a trust bundle but no signer identity in the \
-                             lockfile — reinstall with: nono pull {} --force",
-                            pack_ref, pack_ref
-                        ),
-                    })?,
-            };
-            verify_stored_bundles(&install_dir, &bundle_path, pack_ref, Some(pinned_signer))?;
-        }
+        if !bundle_path.exists() {
+            return Err(nono::NonoError::PackageVerification {
+                package: pack_ref.clone(),
+                reason: format!(
+                    "pack '{}' is missing .nono-trust.bundle - reinstall with: nono pull {} --force",
+                    pack_ref, pack_ref
+                ),
+            });
+        }
+
+        let pinned_signer = locked_pkg
+            .provenance
+            .as_ref()
+            .map(|p| p.signer_identity.as_str())
+            .ok_or_else(|| nono::NonoError::PackageVerification {
+                package: pack_ref.clone(),
+                reason: format!(
+                    "pack '{}' has no signer identity in the lockfile - reinstall with: nono pull {} --force",
+                    pack_ref, pack_ref
+                ),
+            })?;
+        verify_stored_bundles(&install_dir, &bundle_path, pack_ref, Some(pinned_signer))?;
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the package verification logic to explicitly check for the existence of the `.nono-trust.bundle` file. If this file is missing, the verification now fails hard, preventing the use of unverified packages. Additionally, it ensures that a lockfile entry for the package is present before proceeding with artifact and provenance verification.

## GHSA-4pj9-g833-qx53 · lettre

2026-07-28 · RUST · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The `lettre` library, when configured to use the Boring TLS backend, incorrectly inverted the logic for hostname verification. The `set_verify_hostname` and `verify_hostname` methods were passed the `accept_invalid_hostnames` boolean directly, meaning that if `accept_invalid_hostnames` was true (indicating a desire to skip verification), hostname verification was actually enabled, and vice-versa.

### Impact

An attacker could perform a Man-in-the-Middle (MitM) attack against SMTP connections using the Boring TLS backend, even when hostname verification was explicitly requested by the user. This could lead to the interception or modification of sensitive email traffic.

### Key diff

```
-                    config.set_verify_hostname(accept_invalid_hostnames);
+                    config.set_verify_hostname(!accept_invalid_hostnames);

-                    .verify_hostname(*accept_invalid_hostnames)
+                    .verify_hostname(!*accept_invalid_hostnames)
```

### Fix summary

The patch corrects the boolean logic for hostname verification. It now passes the inverted value of `accept_invalid_hostnames` to the `set_verify_hostname` and `verify_hostname` methods, ensuring that hostname verification is correctly enabled when invalid hostnames are not accepted, and disabled when they are.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 27 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Go, C#, PHP, Erlang, Python, Java, RUST, Ruby

## GHSA-w6p7-2fxx-4f44 · github.com/pocket-id/pocket-id/backend

2026-07-28 · Go · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The OIDC refresh token flow did not properly re-validate user authorization status, account disablement, or group restrictions. Once a refresh token was issued, it could be used indefinitely to obtain new access tokens, even if the user's account was disabled, their authorization for the client was revoked, or their group membership no longer allowed access to the client.

### Impact

An attacker could maintain access to a client application using a refresh token even after the associated user account was disabled, the client's authorization was revoked, or the user's group permissions changed, leading to unauthorized access to resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/internal/service/oidc_service.go
+++ b/backend/internal/service/oidc_service.go
@@ -534,6 +534,43 @@ func (s *OidcService) createTokenFromRefreshToken(ctx context.Context, input dto
 		return CreatedTokens{}, &common.OidcInvalidRefreshTokenError{}
 	}
 
+	if storedRefreshToken.User.Disabled {
+		return CreatedTokens{}, &common.OidcInvalidRefreshTokenError{}
+	}
+
+	var authorizedClient model.UserAuthorizedOidcClient
+	err = tx.
+		WithContext(ctx).
+		Where("user_id = ? AND client_id = ?", storedRefreshToken.UserID, input.ClientID).
+		First(&authorizedClient).
+		Error
+...
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces checks during the refresh token flow to verify if the user account is disabled, if the client authorization is still valid, and if the user still belongs to an allowed group for the client. Additionally, when a client authorization is revoked, all associated refresh tokens are now explicitly deleted.
